# Performance and stability

<!-- GOAL12_LEDGER baseline=b79e4cc834970cca69daebffab7df1da7d1e52c4 source=595/518/77/0 examples=99/99/0 -->

This document is the **non-functional ledger** among the four independent
acceptance axes: the behavior gate answers "what was produced", the source
ledger answers where the fixed baseline's capabilities landed, the presentation
ledger answers what the user saw, and this document answers "how much time,
allocation and resident resources producing it cost". Every optimization must
still pass the existing payload, session, complete-copy and virtual-terminal
verbatim gates.

Behavior coverage is in `docs/parity-map.md`, per-source-file disposition is in
`docs/source-parity.md`, the non-functional budget is maintained by this
document `docs/performance.md`, and presentation semantics are in
`docs/presentation-parity.md`. `examples/manifest.sexp` is an auxiliary
inventory of the 99 runnable extension examples, not a fifth ledger. The
`GOAL12_LEDGER` identity above continues to lock the source/example
denominators; the presentation ledger separately locks its own B1-B4
denominators and disposition total with `GOAL13_PRESENTATION_LEDGER`.

## Measurement contract

Single-command entry point:

```sh
./scripts/bench.sh --label local --output /tmp/cadr-performance.json
```

The default parameters are fixed at a 100×40 terminal,
`N ∈ {0, 50, 200, 1000, 5000}`, 1 warm-up and 5 formal samples; the output is
schema version 1 JSON. The formal samples report median and MAD, and every raw
sample is retained.
`--quick` serves development-time smoke only and cannot serve as acceptance
numbers.

Session entries are generated by cycling through a fixed set of five: user
Markdown+CJK/emoji, assistant Markdown, a collapsed read tool block, an
expanded bash tool block, and an image Markdown placeholder+CJK/emoji. There is
no real LLM, network or random input. The hot paths are measured separately:

- `frame_render`: production `render-tui-state → render-frame → virtual-terminal`;
- `key_to_frame`: raw input bytes write out one frame after the input
  decoder/editor;
- `stream_delta_to_frame`: a fixed text delta writes out one frame after agent
  event assembly, and delta/s is reported at the same time;
- `mouse_drag_to_frame`: real SGR press/drag bytes in the alternate screen write
  out one frame after the selection update;
- `wheel_to_frame`: real SGR wheel-up/down bytes in the alternate screen scroll
  in the middle of a long transcript and write out one frame;
- `continuous_alt_input`: 1024 mixed drag and wheel events on a 5000-entry
  transcript, retaining per event the wall latency, allocation, p95/p99/max and
  GC-affected event latency;
- `common_continuous_input`: takes the p95 entry count of the anonymized
  real-session profile as the common tier, splits mouse drag, wheel and editor
  key into three mutually non-diluting 1024-event traces, each retaining its own
  raw latency/allocation samples and enforcing the p99 ≤16ms absolute gate;
- `append_entry`: appends one entry at the tail of a fixed-length history and
  restores the fixture in O(1).

Allocation comes from `sb-ext:get-bytes-consed`; heap occupancy comes from
`sb-kernel:dynamic-usage`; Linux RSS comes from `/proc/self/statm`. Cold start
is started by a standalone Python stdlib PTY harness, which launches the native
`./cadr` in a brand-new temporary `HOME` with no provider credentials in the
environment, times from `Popen` until `cadr v` appears in the first frame, and
then verifies a clean exit with `/quit`. It is "new process to first real TUI
frame", not the weaker `--version` path, and it does not obtain non-portable
fake cold-disk numbers by dropping caches.

The continuous-input measurement temporarily fixes the SBCL nursery at 8MiB
inside an `unwind-protect` and restores the original value on exit. It
deliberately makes the 1024-event run pass through multiple GCs; the
`gc_affected_max_us` in the report is the whole event→frame wall latency the
user actually bears, and `gc_cpu_us` is diagnostic only and never poses as a
precise stop-the-world wall pause.

The complexity label is generated from the log-log slope of the positive-N
points: `<0.35` is constant, `<1.5` is linear, and the rest is
quadratic-or-worse. The label is a regression clue; the gate thresholds will be
fixed in Goal 11 Part E from the post-optimization data and the machine
headroom.

## Before-optimization baseline

Anchor: HEAD `0895199805b3905c8e803f95d0d48ad3329a6b8f`,
`src/cadr-cli/tui.lisp` object
`8457a98cd96959293a173d941702c97b55e6e7bc`; SBCL 2.6.7, x86-64,
machine `Friend-MachineD`. Complete per-sample JSON:
[`benchmarks/goal-v11-before.json`](benchmarks/goal-v11-before.json).

Time is in µs/op; allocation is in bytes/op.

| metric | N=0 | N=50 | N=200 | N=1000 | N=5000 | classification |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| frame time | 800.1 | 8,400.1 | 25,600.5 | 108,002.0 | 488,010.0 | linear (0.884) |
| frame allocation | 134,156.8 | 1,927,867.2 | 6,022,476.8 | 28,295,472 | 139,581,776 | linear growth |
| key→frame time | 2,000.0 | 9,200.2 | 25,600.4 | 102,668.7 | 480,010.0 | linear (0.861) |
| key→frame allocation | 723,761.6 | 2,393,868.8 | 6,490,976.0 | 28,392,656 | 138,620,320 | linear growth |
| stream delta→frame time | 1,000.1 | 8,400.2 | 22,200.4 | 95,201.6 | 466,009.0 | linear (0.878) |
| stream throughput (delta/s) | 999.95 | 119.05 | 45.04 | 10.50 | 2.15 | inverse-linear |
| append time | 0.024 | 0.080 | 0.240 | 1.067 | 5.200 | linear (0.910) |

At 5000 entries an ordinary keypress that does not change the history still
costs about 480ms and allocates about 132MiB; the streaming path can handle only
about 2.15 delta/s. This explains the stutter the user actually feels; it is not
a guess about perception. The absolute time of a single append is small, but the
slope confirms it is O(N), and continuously building a session accumulates to
O(N²).

| N | SBCL heap total | heap delta vs N=0 | RSS total | RSS delta vs N=0 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 35,900,704 | 0 | 64,978,944 | 0 |
| 50 | 35,890,864 | -9,840 | 64,839,680 | -139,264 |
| 200 | 36,010,816 | 110,112 | 65,032,192 | 53,248 |
| 1000 | 36,517,056 | 616,352 | 65,658,880 | 679,936 |
| 5000 | 38,855,280 | 2,954,576 | 67,850,240 | 2,871,296 |

The negative deltas at small N are full-GC and page-residency noise; the
heap/RSS increments at 5000 entries are both about 2.8MiB, and the overall curve
is linear. New process to first real TUI frame is 163,502µs,
MAD 4,518µs (five samples, 156–175ms).

## Fixed TS baseline strategy

The audit target is fixed at `b79e4cc83` (v0.84.4), not the continuously moving upstream
`main`. The conclusion is unambiguous: pi likewise traverses the complete
component tree every frame and has no viewport-before-render, no virtual
scrolling and no persisted history-prefix frame; its advantage comes from
per-component caching, O(1) append, render-request coalescing, and writing only
changed lines to the terminal.

### Process-start lower bound

The 2026-08-04 same-machine `--version` measurement fills this in as follows.
The stable state is repeated startup after both the file cache and the runtime
artifacts are ready; "first" is the first process start of that round. This
command exits immediately after printing the version and **does not include TUI
initialization, first-frame rendering, configuration discovery or interactive
component construction**; it can serve only as a lower-bound reference for
process startup cost and cannot replace the other real-PTY cold-start numbers in
this document.

| `--version` process time | cadr | pi `b79e4cc83` |
|---|---:|---:|
| stable | 0.01s | 0.83s |
| first | 0.05s | 1.72s |

| dimension | baseline behavior and evidence |
|---|---|
| history append | `Container.children` is an array and `addChild()` directly does `push`, i.e. O(1); `packages/tui/src/tui.ts:211-216`. Interactive mode only adds the new message/tool component to `chatContainer`; `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:2942-2962, 3318-3369`. |
| per-component cache | `Markdown` caches the complete rendered lines keyed by source text + width and invalidates them explicitly on change; `packages/tui/src/components/markdown.ts:121-169, 239-244`. `Text` uses the same strategy; `packages/tui/src/components/text.ts:13-57, 99-104`. The bash collapsed preview has a separate width-aware cache; `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/bash-execution.ts:148-166`. |
| streaming invalidation | Each delta rebuilds only the current `AssistantMessageComponent`, leaves the stable history components unchanged, and then requests one frame; `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:2966-2998`. The component itself rebuilds its subtree when its content changes; `packages/coding-agent/src/modes/interactive/components/assistant-message.ts:89-170`. |
| full traversal | `Container.render()` traverses every child every frame and concatenates its lines; `packages/tui/src/tui.ts:235-244`. main-screen explicitly calls `render(width)` on all components first; `packages/tui/src/TuiMainScreen.ts:146-173`. A stable frame is therefore still O(N), and the cache only lowers the per-item constant. |
| viewport/virtual scroll | alt-screen renders the complete document first and only afterwards slices the viewport by `scrollTop`; `packages/tui/src/TuiAltScreen.ts:350-371`. There is no pre-render cropping and no virtual scrolling. |
| differential write | main-screen scans all old and new lines to find the changed range, then writes only the changed lines; `packages/tui/src/TuiMainScreen.ts:260-287, 354-386`. alt-screen likewise writes only changed rows; `packages/tui/src/TuiAltScreen.ts:398-425`. This reduces terminal I/O; it does not eliminate the preceding O(N) render traversal. |
| render coalescing | Duplicate requests are coalesced and scheduled at a 16ms minimum interval; `packages/tui/src/tui.ts:321, 730-770`. |
| alternate-screen input | mouse drag and wheel only update selection/scroll state and request a render; the next frame still renders the complete document, then slices the viewport and differential-writes the changed rows; `packages/tui/src/TuiAltScreen.ts:147-167, 350-425`. |

Part C therefore adopts only the two mechanisms the baseline already has: adding
a width/content-sensitive rendered-line cache for stable entries, and changing
Lisp list tail insertion into an O(1) append that maintains a tail pointer. It
will not add whole-transcript memoization, viewport pruning or virtual
scrolling; these all go beyond the baseline and, under the global principle,
must go through a separate proposal.

### Same-machine structural comparison

Comparison entry point:

```sh
node --expose-gc scripts/goal11-pi-baseline-bench.mjs \
  /tmp/cadr-parity-b79e4cc83 \
  > /tmp/cadr-goal11-pi-baseline.json
```

The script rejects any worktree that is not `b79e4cc83e71…`. The archived object
first runs a clean build of `tui`, `ai build:offline`, `agent` and
`coding-agent` with an empty build cache, then loads the production
`TuiMainScreen`, `TuiAltScreen`, `Editor`, user/assistant, read tool and bash
tool components. The archive simultaneously locks the source object ID, the
SHA-256 of the four loaded `dist/` trees, the `package-lock.json` object ID and
the SHA-256 of the complete installed dependency tree; the whole JSON is
separately locked by SHA-256
`56e09adc50af1c9433111cc018b095c059f99bf7ada366f5d437f64d64b46ad9`.
Every history entry is an independent production component; 100×40, the same N
ladder, 1 warm-up, 5 samples, and fixture construction is not timed. Complete
data is in
[`benchmarks/goal-v11-pi-baseline.json`](benchmarks/goal-v11-pi-baseline.json).

| metric at N=5000 | cadr before | pi `b79e4cc83` | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| frame | 488.0ms | 16.43ms | 29.7× |
| key→frame | 480.0ms | 16.61ms | 28.9× |
| stream delta→frame | 466.0ms | 17.31ms | 26.9× |
| append | 5.200µs, linear | 0.00683µs, constant | 761× at N=5000 |

pi's three frame paths are still linear (log-log exponent 1.050/0.962/0.996) and
append is constant (-0.092). This is not an absolute-throughput
cross-implementation comparison of verbatim-identical runtimes: cadr also drives
its virtual-terminal state, whereas pi writes into an I/O-free mock terminal;
the absolute ratio therefore serves only as an objective same-machine yardstick,
and the algorithmic verdict together with `file:line` is what constrains the
implementation. Even read under that limitation, cadr's 466–488ms at 5000
entries still far exceeds pi's 14–18ms; the problem is a substantial high
constant and O(N) append, not an excuse that "Lisp is inherently slow".

## Optimized results and gates

### Part C — baseline mechanisms restored

The implementation uses only the baseline mechanisms confirmed in Part B:

- `tui-entry` caches the complete rendered lines of stable entries, keyed by
  width, theme object and extension snapshot; streaming, running tool and custom
  tool renderers are not cached. Tool completion, collapse toggling, theme
  change and extension reload all invalidate it. Following Goal 14 B6 the
  implementation lives respectively at
  `src/cadr-cli/interactive/state.lisp:3-34`,
  `entries.lisp:88-95` and `transcript.lisp:812-874`.
- `tui-state` keeps the list tail and the head it belongs to; a normal append
  attaches directly to the tail. The centralized replacement entry point rebuilds
  the tail in step, and if a historical test/fixture does `setf entries`
  directly, the next append performs a single self-repair and does not
  contaminate the old chain. Following Goal 14 B6 the fields live at
  `src/cadr-cli/interactive/state.lisp:36-47` and the centralized
  append/replacement entry point at `entries.lisp:47-86`; production reset still
  goes only through that entry point.

From Part C onward the benchmark's `frame_render` explicitly warms the fixture
once and measures a stable frame; this is a methodological correction that only
becomes meaningful once the cache exists. Before the optimization there was no
rendered-line cache, and a warmed run would still recompute completely; the
archived before numbers therefore remain an equivalent comparison anchor. The
append repetitions were raised at the same time, to keep the O(1) operation from
falling below SBCL's timer resolution.

Complete after JSON:
[`benchmarks/goal-v11-after-part-c.json`](benchmarks/goal-v11-after-part-c.json),
HEAD `56a50ca9cc03…`, TUI object `5b0b4790cd0e…`.

| metric at N=5000 | before | after | improvement | scaling |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| steady frame | 488.01ms / 139.58MB | 4.00ms / 0.72MB | 122× time / 194× allocation | linear 0.884 → constant 0.131 |
| key→frame | 480.01ms / 138.62MB | 4.00ms / 1.38MB | 120× / 101× | linear 0.861 → constant 0.050 |
| stream delta→frame | 466.01ms / 138.63MB | 4.00ms / 1.40MB | 116× / 99× | linear 0.878 → constant 0.066 |
| stream throughput | 2.15 delta/s | 250 delta/s | 116× | — |
| append | 5.200µs | 0.020µs | 260× | linear 0.910 → constant -0.021 |

Cold start is 159.1ms (MAD 3.0ms), the same order of magnitude as the before
value of 163.5ms; the cache was not used to fake a startup gain. The heap/RSS
increment of the unrendered 5000-entry fixture rose from about 2.95/2.87MiB to
3.18/3.17MiB, coming from the cache-metadata slot on each entry; the long-term
residency cost and reclamation behavior of the cache after rendering is measured
separately by Part D.

### Part D — ten-hour deterministic stability run

The entry point `scripts/goal11-stability.sh` uses the production faux provider,
the real agent/session persistence paths, TUI event projection and per-event
virtual-terminal rendering, and at two rounds per minute interprets 1200 rounds
as 10 hours of interaction volume. It forces a full GC at rounds
0/300/600/900/1200 and records heap/RSS, GC pause, FDs and Lisp/OS threads; it
also generates at least 10MiB of real v3 JSONL and, for `load-session`,
`resume-session` and `continue-session` each, records the heap/RSS peak with a
1ms sampler. Complete committed data is in
[`benchmarks/goal-v11-stability.json`](benchmarks/goal-v11-stability.json).

| stability metric | result |
|---|---:|
| faux turns / rendered agent events | 1200 / 14,400 |
| retained heap, turn 0 → 1200 | 35.86MiB → 78.17MiB; exponent 0.966 |
| full-GC pause, turn 0 → max | 52.0ms → 80.0ms |
| FD / Lisp threads / OS threads | 5 / 1 / 2 at every checkpoint and after cleanup |
| post-cleanup heap vs pre-run | -0.09MiB |
| JSONL size / entries | 10,507,435 bytes / 2400 |
| load / resume / continue latency | 328 / 268 / 276ms |
| sampled peak RSS above operation baseline | 99.8 / 98.7 / 98.5MiB |

The growth comes from the 2400 messages/entries still normally referenced by the
agent, the session, the TUI cache and the faux prompt cache; the curve is linear
rather than a leak, and after the whole scenario is released heap, FDs and
threads all return to the baseline. GC pause did not degrade monotonically with
the checkpoints. All three load paths for a 10MiB session are below 0.33s, with
about 73.1MiB retained heap, and no stability defect requiring a change to
observable behavior was found.

The scenario audit instead found that the in-memory append of agent messages and
session entries still uses list `nconc`, which is cumulatively O(N²) just like
the TUI that Part C already fixed. The fixed TS baseline uses
`Agent.state.messages.push()` (`packages/agent/src/agent.ts:541`) and
`SessionManager.fileEntries.push()` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/session-manager.ts:1044-1048`)
respectively. The same baseline mechanism is therefore carried over, maintaining
a private tail for both; after the public list is replaced wholesale the next
append performs one safety repair. On the old object the agent append exponent
was 0.994 and 21.593µs at N=20000; the new implementation has exponent -0.035
and 0.110µs at N=20000 (196×). Session append is dominated by the roughly 0.1ms
fixed cost of generating a random ID per entry, but at N=20000 it still fell
from 135.394µs to 109.678µs; a structural gate locks the cons tail linkage
directly, so that timing noise cannot mask a regression.

## Performance gates

`scripts/goal11-performance-gate.py` applies a hard gate to the complete
performance/stability JSON.
The thresholds deliberately leave an order of magnitude of headroom for
CI/machine variation, but they still break through this goal's old defects: at
N=5000 frame/key/stream/mouse/wheel must each not exceed 50ms, frame/mouse/wheel
allocation must not exceed 16MiB, and stream must be at least 20 delta/s; the
continuous alternate-screen run must record exactly 1024 events, p99 must not
exceed 150ms, GC-affected max must not exceed 500ms, and when the allocation
crosses the nursery a GC must actually be observed; TUI/agent/session append
exponents must be less than 0.35 and cold start must not exceed 750ms;
1200-turn heap must not exceed 128MiB and its exponent must not exceed 1.30,
full GC must not exceed 500ms, and FD/thread must be constant point by point;
10MiB load/resume/continue must each not exceed 5s, and the sampled heap/RSS
peaks must each not exceed the baseline by 512MiB.

On the same machine a fixed-baseline gate is also applied to the five-round
1024-event traces for mouse, wheel and default GC: the upper bound on
median/p95/p99 is
`max(2 × pi_q, pi_q + max(5ms, 6 × pi_run_MAD_q))`, and max is diagnostic only.
When the candidate machine name is not the archive machine `Friend-MachineD`,
that relative gate explicitly reports
`informational-host-mismatch`; it will not pass off another machine's absolute
microsecond numbers as comparable data. The original 50/150/500ms absolute
budgets still run across machines, but they are equally affected by host
performance and cannot be called host-independent.

### Fifth A/B — continuous alternate-screen input

The old benchmark measured only ordinary frames, keyboard and stream deltas; the
alt-screen tests asserted only scrolling and selection semantics, with no wall
latency, allocation or GC tail for continuous mouse events. All functional
checks could therefore be fully green while real dragging and wheeling still
rescanned every ANSI line in a long document on every frame. This is a missing
gate dimension, not an existing assertion that happened to miss one constant.

The pre-optimization diagnostic obtained by quantifying first was already
unambiguous after running only 8 events at N=5000 with a fixed 8MiB nursery:
median 896.041ms, p99/max 932.060ms, median allocation
202,858,216 bytes/event, and 8/8 events crossing a GC; the default 1024-event
run had still not finished after more than two minutes and was aborted
deliberately, and it cannot be disguised as a complete result.

The fixed baseline is still full document render + viewport slice + changed-row
write, and it is not permitted to take the occasion to introduce virtual
scrolling or transcript memoization. This round eliminates only equivalent
constant costs on the Lisp hot path: stable lines no longer repeatedly construct
reset/copy, scroll/selection no longer force a full clear,
`visible-width` scans the original string directly and is differentially
compared against the old grapheme semantics by property tests, and image prefix
detection changes from two generic substring searches per line to a
same-semantics prefix decision made from the ESC position. The latter has the
same fast/slow prefix semantics as the baseline `isImageLine`, but avoids SBCL's
generic `search` taking up most of a frame on an ordinary ANSI line.

The final committed 1024-event run (HEAD `15bc6ae084d3…`) has completed: at
N=5000 the mouse/wheel medians are both 36.001ms, with allocation
2,832,352/3,668,816 bytes; continuous median 36.001ms, p95 48.001ms, p99
52.001ms, max 68.002ms, and the max over the 447 GC-affected events is 68.002ms.
Relative to the 8-event diagnostic, median
latency fell about 24.9× and
median allocation about 54×; the default scale now runs to completion and
retains all 1024 raw samples. Cold start median 157.428ms (MAD 3.222ms), TUI
append exponent 0.029. The complete JSON is
[`benchmarks/goal-v11-final.json`](benchmarks/goal-v11-final.json), SHA-256
`1d2d3d1faac43006e56b08a505487035c0d8a6dffdc0b52ab069a45c0d0d8f1e`.

### Goal 12 A0 — paired continuous-input baseline

The old `14–18ms` comparison drove only `TuiMainScreen` and cannot serve as an
alt-screen mouse/wheel yardstick. A0 changes both sides to go through the
production raw-input handler and the production render request, and to flush the
requested frame immediately; only the common 16ms scheduler wait is excluded.
The fixed conditions are 100×40, N=5000, five brand-new fixtures, and 1024
completely identical events per round (342 drag, 341 wheel-up, 341 wheel-down),
with trace SHA-256
`2dfea100e8e8825a96ea55795c39e6cea2f265c6aa1a4b98f4edba8b4e5edb61`.
The baseline path evidence is
`packages/tui/src/TuiAltScreen.ts:73-84,147-180,234-277,350-425`
and `packages/tui/src/tui.ts:730-770`; the cadr paths are
`src/cadr-cli/interactive/controller.lisp:2050-2160` and
`src/cadr-tui/renderer/lifecycle-input.lisp:619-642`.

Goal 14 B6 only moved interactive forms; the performance harness's
compatibility field `tui_object_id` now records the Git object of
`interactive/controller.lisp`, and the complete presentation behavior is still
locked jointly by the deterministic gates of the controller, frame, transcript
and renderer.

| paired N=5000 metric | pi `b79e4cc83` | cadr `82db71b8c` | cadr / pi |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| mouse event→frame median | 276.150ms | 32.000ms | 0.12× |
| wheel event→frame median | 297.999ms | 36.000ms | 0.12× |
| continuous median | 315.576ms | 32.001ms | 0.10× |
| continuous p95 | 347.536ms | 40.001ms | 0.12× |
| continuous p99 | 365.552ms | 48.001ms | 0.13× |
| continuous max | 427.549ms | 68.002ms | 0.16× |

Allocation is not disguised as the same kind of byte: cadr's SBCL
`get-bytes-consed` is an exact allocation count, and the
continuous median is 2,865,296 bytes/event; pi's V8 inspector sampled
allocation estimate is 112,307,351 bytes/event, with a separately measured
retained-heap delta of 47,290 bytes/event and high-water delta of 73,522
bytes/event. They only prove that both sides really sample allocation; they are
not used for a byte-for-byte gate.

Conclusion: the fixed baseline is likewise an O(N) full document render →
viewport slice → changed-row write, and on this synthetic N=5000 trace it is
6.3–9.9× slower than cadr. cadr's 32–48ms
bulk latency is still humanly perceptible, but it is not a mechanism added
relative to the baseline nor an order-of-magnitude regression; going on to add
virtual scrolling/transcript memoization that the baseline does not have would
instead violate the project's principles. A0 is therefore closed, the existing
absolute stress gate is retained, and the same-machine baseline-derived gate
described above is added. The candidate's complete data is
[`benchmarks/goal-v12-a0-cadr.json`](benchmarks/goal-v12-a0-cadr.json), SHA-256
`8f85b41714f142f721218009bf40524e0ff842c4c6c3d76dda486595ca05d414`.

## Goal 13 C3 — absolute interaction latency

### Real-session tiers

`scripts/goal13-session-scale-profile.py` reads the active branch of every local
JSONL session and emits aggregate counts only. It deliberately excludes file
names, session/entry IDs, cwd, timestamps, provider/model, and message content;
its fixture includes a secret marker and rejects any leak. Historical
`parentId:false` is handled as the production null/root representation instead
of silently discarding old sessions.

The 2026-08-02 profile contains 45/45 valid sessions and zero rejected files:

| percentile | min | p50 | p90 | p95 | p99 | max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| projected TUI entries | 0 | 5 | 48 | 58 | 96 | 96 |

The tiers are therefore `typical=5`, `common=58`, `heavy=96`, and the retained
synthetic stress tier `5000`. The checked aggregate is
[`benchmarks/goal-v13-session-scale.json`](benchmarks/goal-v13-session-scale.json),
SHA-256 `95ce7f4a215e5d5e30f9cc8231958d522e912154fd8ffcbfba74f5316867a7b1`.
The release gate uses the checked aggregate, not the build machine's mutable
home directory.

### Bottleneck and optimization

Before C3, `render-tui-state` already reused stable entry render results, but
alternate-screen `prepare-alt-document` still stripped OSC-133 prefixes,
measured ANSI/grapheme width, and clipped every line on every mouse or wheel
event. The fresh pre-change run at `cd94d0b01` measured N=5000 continuous
median/p95/p99 `36.000/48.001/52.001ms` and 2,847,360 bytes/event. Its complete
samples are archived in
[`benchmarks/goal-v13-c3-before.json`](benchmarks/goal-v13-c3-before.json),
SHA-256 `06d7faeb91ad7164b1d4258d6c18d3b6f1469b9bcfc09d22989a413d88583066`.
Its five real-PTY cold-start samples were measured from an isolated image
built at that exact source revision; median 158.00ms, MAD 0.39ms.

The renderer now retains the immediately preceding raw line list, prepared
document, and width. A new frame reuses prepared strings for the maximal
identity-stable prefix and only performs OSC stripping, width measurement, and
clipping from the first dirty line onward
(`src/cadr-tui/renderer/lifecycle-input.lisp:13-16`;
`src/cadr-tui/renderer/alt-screen.lisp:3-57,123-124`). Width changes force
a full rebuild. The cache is one generation only, so it is bounded; the full
logical document remains available to viewport cropping, selection/copy, and
alternate-screen exit. This is memoized layout, not a presentation change.

The regression `GOAL13-ALT-SCREEN-REUSES-ONLY-THE-UNCHANGED-PREPARED-PREFIX`
proves exact equality with a forced full preparation, suffix invalidation, and
width invalidation. `GOAL13-ALT-SCREEN-PREPARED-CACHE-KEEPS-THE-FULL-DOCUMENT-CONTRACT`
proves exit restoration contains the updated complete document. Every existing
base-screen, overlay, payload, wire, and event gate remains byte-identical.

### Final measurements and gates

The committed implementation is `7da79c376`; renderer object
`6b56fa43686cd04e44fb84b5d1a2ccfb0f8495b8`. Each common trace contains 1024
separately timed production input→requested frame operations under the forced
8MiB nursery:

| tier / input | median | p95 | p99 | max | median allocation |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| common N=58 mouse drag | <4ms timer quantum | 4.000ms | 4.001ms | 4.001ms | 180,552 B |
| common N=58 wheel | <4ms timer quantum | 4.000ms | 4.000ms | 4.001ms | 69,888 B |
| common N=58 editor key | <4ms timer quantum | 4.000ms | 4.001ms | 8.000ms | 1,124,488 B |
| stress N=5000 mixed | <4ms timer quantum | 4.000ms | 8.000ms | 12.000ms | 887,248 B |

The synthetic N=5000 p99 improved `52.001ms → 8.000ms` (6.5×) and median
allocation improved `2,847,360 → 887,248` bytes (3.2×). It now clears the 16ms
target even at 86× the real common tier; further virtual scrolling is not
needed to close this bottleneck. Cold start is 154.57ms (five real-PTY
samples, MAD 2.16ms) and the existing
1200-turn/10MiB stability budgets remain unchanged.

`scripts/goal11-performance-gate.py` now emits distinct failures:

- `absolute interaction target: common <kind> p99 exceeds 16ms` for any of the
  three common traces;
- `fixed baseline regression: ...` for the same-machine pi comparison.

It also requires `renderer_object_id`, exact 1024-sample traces, recomputed
quantiles/allocation/GC summaries, final selection/viewport/editor state, and
the privacy-safe scale profile contract. The final full artifact is
[`benchmarks/goal-v13-c3-final.json`](benchmarks/goal-v13-c3-final.json),
SHA-256 `96350ee3588e0143d745b1af2f0c295f542b5c6c0c31693b65710dcea0ed11f6`.
The checked artifact is replayed by the synthetic gate regression and by
`verify.sh`; a missing or null cold-start block now fails before release.

### Sustained mixed interaction tail

The original C3 gate timed isolated events and 1024-event bursts. That proves
ordinary event-to-frame latency, but it cannot detect a pause that appears only
after repeated drag selection, wheel input, and editing. The release benchmark
now adds a real-time 180-second trace at 60 events/second over the checked
`common=58` session tier: exactly 10,800 production input-to-requested-frame
operations, split evenly among drag, wheel-up, wheel-down, and editor
insert/backspace. The gate recomputes every tail statistic from the complete
sample vector and rejects a trace shorter than three minutes.

The 2026-08-03 run measured:

| measurement | result | gate |
|---|---:|---:|
| event-to-frame p99.9 | 8.000ms | ≤100ms |
| maximum event-to-frame pause | 20.001ms | ≤500ms |
| GC-affected events | 91 / 10,800 | reported |
| maximum GC-affected event latency | 20.001ms | reported |
| GC runtime-counter p99.9 / max | 15.491 / 15.491ms | recomputed |
| GC frequency | 30.33/min | recomputed |
| total GC runtime | 167.196ms | reported |
| retained heap after final full GC | +241,520 B | ≤8MiB |
| post-GC RSS | +581,632 B | ≤8MiB |

`sb-ext:*gc-run-time*` supplies the GC runtime-counter deltas; it is not
misrepresented as an independently sampled stop-the-world wall clock. The
corresponding user-observed wall latency is the complete
`gc_affected_samples_us` event-to-frame vector. Raw heap samples show the
normal nursery sawtooth: the last 30 seconds ended 19,924,288 bytes above their
first heap sample while RSS grew 2,097,152 bytes. A final full collection then
left only 241,520 retained heap bytes and 581,632 RSS bytes over the pre-trace
baseline, so the curve converges rather than accumulating per event.

`ContinuousInputGateTest.test_sustained_mixed_interaction_is_required_and_gated`
rejects a missing trace, a forged tail summary, short duration, threshold
breach, inconsistent GC accounting, or a claimed convergence that disagrees
with post-GC retained state. `verify.sh` runs this three-minute trace inside
its isolated Goal 11 performance stage on every release verification.

## Goal 20 Part O remaining-path profiles

The four paired paths still above 1.0× after probes 7a–7d are captured
in [`benchmarks/goal20-part-o-remaining-sprof.md`](benchmarks/goal20-part-o-remaining-sprof.md)
by `scripts/goal20-profile.sh`. Diagnostic only; not a gate. The standing
runbook is [`profiling.md`](profiling.md). N2.9 asked
for package-level profiles next to the totals; the flat reports live in
that file so a later ledger rewrite cannot wipe them.

## Goal 20 Part H unpairable paths

Which production Lisp files can currently falsify "no worse than the
baseline", which are booked unpairable (single reference), and which
still block Part K, is generated in
[`acceptance/goal-v20-part-h.md`](acceptance/goal-v20-part-h.md) §6
by `scripts/goal20-unpaired-paths.py`. Do not hand-edit those tables.
